# <b>{ "176" } of { "388" }</b> capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } honestly labelled Modelled, { "4" } partner-owned. Among the checks: all { "666" } SGP4 (Simplified General Perturbations { "4" }) vectors of AIAA (American Institute of Aeronautics and Astronautics) 2006-6753 to { "4.12" } mm, and the Cowell force model to { "0.08" } m against Orekit { "12.2" }.

# How a run flows. A scenario TOML file (a kind, a seed and its parameters) goes into the engine, kshana { $version }, through the api::run_toml dispatch over { "87" } scenario kinds, deterministic from scenario, seed and engine version. The engine writes result.json, chart.svg, report.html and report.json, a table.csv for the kinds that define one, and on request SP3, CCSDS OMM and OEM, CZML, KML, GeoJSON, STK and SigMF exports; a suite writes study.json and study.html. Those files feed Kshana Studio in the browser, an AI assistant through the kshana-mcp server, and continuous integration.
seg-76e3ec458441 = Sådan løber en kørsel. En scenariefil i TOML (en type, et seed og dens parametre) går ind i motoren, kshana { $version }, gennem udsendelsen api::run_toml over { "87" } scenarietyper, deterministisk ud fra scenarie, seed og motorversion. Motoren skriver result.json, chart.svg, report.html og report.json, en table.csv for de typer, der definerer en, og efter anmodning eksporter til SP3, CCSDS OMM og OEM, CZML, KML, GeoJSON, STK og SigMF; en pakke skriver study.json og study.html. Disse filer føder Kshana Studio i browseren, en AI-assistent gennem serveren kshana-mcp og kontinuerlig integration.

# <b>{ "176" } of { "388" }</b> capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } honestly labelled Modelled. Each row of the verification matrix names a capability, the oracle it is checked against, the test that runs the check, and the label that follows. Continuous integration (CI) makes it impossible to call a capability VALIDATED without an independent external oracle.
seg-707e2cf795f6 = <b>{ "176" } af { "388" }</b> funktioner valideret mod uafhængige eksterne referencer; { "208" } ærligt mærket som modelleret. Hver række i verifikationsmatrixen nævner en funktion, den reference, den kontrolleres mod, den test, der kører kontrollen, og den etiket, der følger. Kontinuerlig integration (CI) gør det umuligt at kalde en funktion VALIDATED uden en uafhængig ekstern reference.

# How a capability earns its label. Each row of the verification matrix names a capability, the oracle it is checked against, the test that runs the check in continuous integration, and the label that follows. A row may be VALIDATED only with an independent external oracle; otherwise it is MODELLED, or PARTNER when a hardware partner owns it. Example: SGP4/SDP4, Cowell 6-DOF + perturbations, batch/sequential OD, oracle AIAA 2006-6753 SGP4 verification vectors, test tests/sgp4_verification.rs, label VALIDATED. Live counts: { "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER of { "388" } rows. In all, { "176" } capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } more are honestly labelled MODELLED and { "4" } are PARTNER-owned.
seg-2f81cf391eae = Sådan optjener en funktion sin etiket. Hver række i verifikationsmatrixen nævner en funktion, den reference, den kontrolleres mod, den test, der kører kontrollen i kontinuerlig integration, og den etiket, der følger. En række må kun være VALIDATED med en uafhængig ekstern reference; ellers er den MODELLED, eller PARTNER, når en hardwarepartner ejer den. Eksempel: SGP4/SDP4, Cowell 6-DOF + forstyrrelser, batch/sekventiel OD, reference AIAA 2006-6753 SGP4-verifikationsvektorer, test tests/sgp4_verification.rs, etiket VALIDATED. Aktuelle tal: { "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER af { "388" } rækker. I alt { "176" } funktioner valideret mod uafhængige eksterne referencer; { "208" } flere er ærligt mærket MODELLED, og { "4" } ejes af en hardwarepartner (PARTNER).

# Kshana's architecture: one open engine at the centre, kshana { $version } under the AGPL-3.0, with api::run_toml, a typed dispatch over every kind, and the verification ledger of { "388" } capabilities ({ "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER). Around it, every surface runs the same engine: Command line (cargo install kshana); Rust library (cargo add kshana); Python (pip install kshana); WebAssembly + Kshana Studio (npm install kshana); MCP server (cargo install kshana-mcp); Docker image (ghcr.io/ashfordeou/kshana-mcp); JetBrains plugin (Marketplace: "Kshana"). Below it, Kshana Pro, a proprietary overlay that depends on the open engine as a library and never forks it, and adds no physical model.
seg-9d69cf43cd40 = Kshanas arkitektur: én åben motor i midten, kshana { $version } under AGPL-3.0, med api::run_toml, en typet fordeling over hver kind og verifikationsregistret med { "388" } funktioner ({ "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER). Rundt om den kører hver flade den samme motor: Kommandolinje (cargo install kshana); Rust-bibliotek (cargo add kshana); Python (pip install kshana); WebAssembly + Kshana Studio (npm install kshana); MCP-server (cargo install kshana-mcp); Docker-billede (ghcr.io/ashfordeou/kshana-mcp); JetBrains-plugin (Marketplace: "Kshana"). Nedenunder ligger Kshana Pro, et proprietært overlay, der afhænger af den åbne motor som bibliotek og aldrig forgrener den, og som ikke tilføjer nogen fysisk model.

# Kshana's architecture in layers. Five front doors (command line, Python, JavaScript and WebAssembly, the MCP server and the JetBrains plugin) reach one api::run_toml, a typed dispatch over { "87" } kinds. Beneath it sit eight domain layers: time and frames, clocks and timing, inertial and fusion, GNSS and integrity, astrodynamics, LEO PNT, Moon, Mars and deep space, and missions and studies, on a shared core, all cross-referenced by the verification module, the machine-checked matrix of { "388" } capabilities that is the single source of truth for every label.
seg-9bc9a971bdb4 = Kshanas arkitektur i lag. Fem indgange (kommandolinje, Python, JavaScript og WebAssembly, MCP-serveren og JetBrains-pluginnet) når én api::run_toml, en typet fordeling over { "87" } kinds. Nedenunder ligger otte domænelag: tid og referencesystemer, ure og tidssynkronisering, inerti og fusion, GNSS og integritet, astrodynamik, LEO PNT, Månen, Mars og dybt rum samt missioner og studier, oven på en fælles kerne, alle krydsrefereret af verifikationsmodulet, den maskinkontrollerede matrix med { "388" } funktioner, som er den ene sandhedskilde for hver mærkning.

# Three independent tools, pre-registered (0123cee1) before any fixture or oracle output existed, tolerances fixed then. ({ "1" }) ARAIM: Stanford MAAST for ARAIM { "2" } (commit ab70e2a3, BSD-3) mhss_raim_baseline_v5.m, run as a separate program under GNU Octave { "8.4" } with a driver-side unique() compatibility shim (disclosed, no algorithm change), at the ADD TOL_PL of { "0.05" } m: the Rust reproduces the ADD reference implementation on { "270" } real cases. Disclosed: the Kshana ADD path was written after the pre-registration, following the ADD as MAAST implements it; an independent hold-out by the reviewer ({ "270" } new user geometries and a second integrity support message, MAAST re-run against the frozen code) passed at the same bar. ({ "2" }) Snapshot RAIM: RTKLIB v2.4.2-p13 (BSD-2) estpos/valsol/raim_fde in a separate C harness for detection and exclusion; the slope PL from RTKLIB matinv/xyz2enu plus SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2, where both sides evaluate the same closed form (Brown; Parkinson and Axelrad), so the PL check covers the implementation, not the formula. ({ "3" }) SBAS: Stanford MAAST (commit 7d32b049) usr_vhpl, unmodified, on MAAST own recorded WAAS broadcasts: L1 levels within { "1e-4" } m after the K rescaling and an equal protected set. The same comparisons produced the findings stated in the capability: the uniform-sigma ARAIM functions are not ADD-conformant, and the L5 set equality fails on { "308" } pairs with no GEO in view (the levels on the { "3267" } pairs both tools protect agree to { "1e-13" } m). The DO-229E/DO-316 K-factors are transcribed constants and the real geometry is an input; neither is counted as an oracle. { "0.30" } revision: snapshot_raim protection levels moved from the radial to the geodetic local level (up to { "0.31" } m HPL and { "0.14" } m VPL at { "16" } deg N)

({ "1" }) ARAIM: Stanford MAAST for ARAIM { "2" } (commit ab70e2a3, BSD-3) mhss_raim_baseline_v5.m, kørt som et separat program under GNU Octave { "8.4" } med en kompatibilitets-shim for unique() i driveren (oplyst, ingen ændring af algoritmen), ved ADD TOL_PL på { "0.05" } m: Rust-koden gengiver ADD's referenceimplementering på { "270" } reelle tilfælde. Oplyst: Kshanas ADD-vej blev skrevet efter præregistreringen og følger ADD, som MAAST implementerer den; et uafhængigt tilbageholdt datasæt fra reviewer ({ "270" } nye brugergeometrier og en anden integritetsunderstøttende meddelelse, MAAST kørt igen mod den frosne kode) bestod ved samme tærskel. ({ "2" }) Snapshot-RAIM: RTKLIB v2.4.2-p13 (BSD-2) estpos/valsol/raim_fde i en separat C-harness til detektion og udelukkelse; hældnings-PL fra RTKLIB matinv/xyz2enu plus SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2, hvor begge sider evaluerer den samme lukkede form (Brown; Parkinson og Axelrad), så PL-kontrollen dækker implementeringen og ikke formlen. ({ "3" }) SBAS: Stanford MAAST (commit 7d32b049) usr_vhpl, uændret, på MAAST's egne optagede WAAS-udsendelser: L1-niveauer inden for { "1e-4" } m efter omskaleringen af K og en lige stor beskyttet mængde. # Closed-form identities and internal consistency; NO external oracle is claimed. The limb half angle is checked against asin(R_earth/r_orbit) and the geometry against it; the free-space loss, the DOP kernel and the SGP4 propagation this composes are each externally validated in their own rows and this row does not borrow their status. WHY MODELLED, and it is the transmit pattern: the gain at angle is a uniformly illuminated circular aperture, the Airy pattern, while a real GPS L1 antenna is a twelve-element helical array with a shaped main lobe peaked off-boresight to even out power across the Earth disc and sidelobes that are not Airy. Published measured patterns exist and are not vendored here, so orderings and orders of magnitude carry, while the dB of any single satellite does not. A FIRST IMPLEMENTATION OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING: the Airy expression is valid only in the forward hemisphere, its argument goes as sin(theta), and at theta near { "180" } deg it wraps around and returns FULL BORESIGHT GAIN behind the aperture - so the two strongest links in the first report were satellites pointing their antennas away from the Moon, and they were the only two that cleared the threshold. Behind the aperture the model now applies a flat back-lobe floor, stated as a bound rather than a fabricated pattern shape. Deliberately absent, each making the budget OPTIMISTIC: no ionospheric or tropospheric loss on the limb-grazing rays, no polarisation, pointing or implementation loss, and a spherical Earth with no refractive extension. The Moon position is an INPUT, not an ephemeris lookup, because the quantity under test is the link and the beam geometry. Upgrading this row to Validated needs a measured transmit pattern and LuGRE normal points to check against; neither is in the repository and neither is invented here. { "0.30" } round { "2" }, a finding (stays MODELLED): a measured-input link path (real SP3 positions, per-block transmit power, tabulated transmit and receive patterns, acquisition and tracking hysteresis) was checked against Montenbruck et al. { "2023" } Table { "3" } for GENESIS at { "6000" } km (pre-registered 5b414f0a): zenith-antenna means agree on { "5" } of { "6" } within one dB of the paper sensitivity; nadir-antenna means are { "1.0" } to { "2.4" } satellites higher than printed, plausibly because the paper used azimuth-dependent GPS side-lobe patterns that are not published. The lunar-distance figures of this row still use the Airy stand-in. { "0.30" }+ D7, a finding (stays MODELLED): the restated quantity, the C/N0 DIFFERENCE between Block IIR/IIR-M satellites tracked at one epoch, predicted with the NAVCEN per-SVN measured L1 patterns (two-dimensional, yaw-steering frame) against the LuGRE flight C/N0 (pre-registered 768cb62b: { "99" } % visible, pair RMS <= { "3.0" } dB, median <= { "2.0" } dB, at least { "50" } records and { "30" } pairs) has too little data: the { "26" } qualifying transit and lunar-orbit epochs hold { "15" } tracked IIR/IIR-M records (mostly IIF and III are tracked) and one pair. All { "15" } are predicted visible and the single pair's residual is -0.06 dB; nothing is claimed
seg-bed2679f88e5 = Identiteter på lukket form og intern konsistens; INGEN ekstern uafhængig reference påstås. Limbens halve vinkel kontrolleres mod asin(R_earth/r_orbit) og geometrien mod den; fririumstabet, DOP-kernen og SGP4-propageringen, som dette sammensætter, er hver især eksternt valideret i deres egne rækker, og denne række låner ikke deres status. HVORFOR MODELLED, og det er sendemønstret: forstærkningen ved en vinkel er en ensartet belyst cirkulær apertur, Airy-mønstret, mens en reel GPS L1-antenne er et spiralformet array med tolv elementer med en formet hovedlobe, der topper uden for boresigtet for at udjævne effekten over Jordens skive, og sidelober, der ikke er Airy. Publicerede målte mønstre findes og er ikke medleveret her, så rækkefølger og størrelsesordener holder, mens dB for en enkelt satellit ikke gør. EN FØRSTE IMPLEMENTERING AF DENNE RÆKKE VAR FORKERT PÅ EN MÅDE, DER ER VÆRD AT REGISTRERE: Airy-udtrykket gælder kun i den forreste halvkugle, dets argument går som sin(theta), og ved theta nær { "180" } deg slår det rundt og returnerer FULD BORESIGTSFORSTÆRKNING bag aperturen — så de to stærkeste link i den første rapport var satellitter, der pegede deres antenner væk fra Månen, og de var de eneste to, der klarede tærsklen. Bag aperturen anvender modellen nu et fladt gulv for bagloben, angivet som en grænse frem for et opdigtet mønster. Bevidst fraværende, og hver gør budgettet OPTIMISTISK: intet ionosfærisk eller troposfærisk tab på de stråler, der strejfer limben, intet tab ved polarisation, pejling eller implementering og en kugleformet Jord uden refraktiv udvidelse. Månens position er et INPUT, ikke et opslag i en efemeride, fordi størrelsen under test er linket og strålegeometrien. At opgradere denne række til validerede kræver et målt sendemønster og LuGRE-normalpunkter at kontrollere mod; ingen af delene findes i repositoriet, og ingen af delene er opfundet her. { "0.30" } runde { "2" }, et fund (forbliver MODELLED): en linksti med målte inputs (reelle SP3-positioner, sendeeffekt pr. blok, tabulerede send- og modtagemønstre, hysterese ved erhvervelse og sporing) blev kontrolleret mod Montenbruck et al. { "2023" } tabel { "3" } for GENESIS ved { "6000" } km (præregistreret 5b414f0a): middelværdier for zenitantenner stemmer på { "5" } af { "6" } inden for én dB af artiklens følsomhed; middelværdier for nadirantenner er { "1.0" } til { "2.4" } satellitter højere end trykt, sandsynligvis fordi artiklen brugte azimutafhængige GPS-sidelobemønstre, som ikke er publiceret. Tallene ved månens afstand i denne række bruger stadig Airy som stand-in. { "0.30" }+ D7, et fund (forbliver MODELLED): den omformulerede størrelse, C/N0-FORSKELLEN mellem Block IIR/IIR-M-satellitter sporet ved én epoke, forudsagt med NAVCEN's målte L1-mønstre pr. SVN (todimensionale, i yaw-styringssystemet) mod LuGRE-flyvningens C/N0 (præregistreret 768cb62b: { "99" } % synlige, RMS for par <= { "3.0" } dB, median <= { "2.0" } dB, mindst { "50" } poster og { "30" } par) har for få data: de { "26" } kvalificerende transit- og månebaneepoker rummer { "15" } sporede IIR/IIR-M-poster (mest IIF og III spores) og ét par. # Two internal oracles, no external dataset. ({ "1" }) Closed-form round trip: the minimum detectable bias is produced by inverting the non-central chi-square tail on the non-centrality (raim::pbias) and is verified by feeding the resulting non-centrality back through raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf at the monitor's own threshold, which must return P_md. ({ "2" }) Injection vs analysis: a bias is written into the RF estimate of one axis and cross_raim::run_cross_raim is re-run, so the statistic compared against the analytic non-centrality is the one the monitor computed, not a re-derivation. A seeded Monte-Carlo of the statistic itself is carried in the tests as a third check; it is deliberately NOT in the report, because a sampled estimate would be slower and not reproducible bit-for-bit. ExternalDataset is declined: the quantity is the detection power of THIS monitor at THIS scenario's sigma allocation, and the sigma magnitudes the MDB is scaled by are Modelled representative inputs, not measurements. ReferenceImpl was also considered and declined — the Monte-Carlo samples the same statistic the analysis describes, so it catches transcription and coefficient errors but is not an independent implementation of the monitor. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered 0135a9e4), blocked (stays MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduce the report threshold (within { "5.4e-12" }), per-axis MDB, power curve, crossing multiple and ramp time-to-detect (within { "5.3e-10" }) on five configurations; the injected-fault statistics have no independent oracle and the sigma magnitudes are Modelled inputs, so the scoping awaits an owner decision
seg-b6b8dec4dcff = To interne uafhængige referencer, intet eksternt datasæt. ({ "1" }) Rundtur i lukket form: den mindste detekterbare bias frembringes ved at invertere halen af den ikke-centrale chi-i-anden på ikke-centraliteten (raim::pbias) og kontrolleres ved at føre den resulterende ikke-centralitet tilbage gennem raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf ved monitorens egen tærskel, som skal returnere P_md. ({ "2" }) Indsprøjtning mod analyse: en bias skrives ind i RF-estimatet for én akse, og cross_raim::run_cross_raim køres igen, så den statistik, der sammenlignes med den analytiske ikke-centralitet, er den, monitoren selv beregnede, ikke en genafledning. En seedet Monte Carlo af selve statistikken er med i testene som en tredje kontrol; den er bevidst IKKE i rapporten, fordi et stikprøveestimat ville være langsommere og ikke kunne gengives bit for bit. ExternalDataset afvises: størrelsen er detektionsstyrken for DENNE monitor ved DETTE scenaries sigmafordeling, og de sigmastørrelser, MDB skaleres med, er modellerede repræsentative input, ikke målinger. ReferenceImpl blev også overvejet og afvist — Monte Carlo udtager den samme statistik, som analysen beskriver, så den fanger afskrivnings- og koefficientfejl, men er ikke en uafhængig implementering af monitoren. { "0.30" } runde { "2" } (præregistreret 0135a9e4), blokeret (forbliver MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq gengiver rapportens tærskel (inden for { "5.4e-12" }), MDB pr. akse, kurven for styrke, krydsningsmultiplum og tid til detektion for rampen (inden for { "5.3e-10" }) på fem konfigurationer; statistikkerne for indsprøjtede fejl har ingen uafhængig reference, og sigmastørrelserne er modellerede input, så afgrænsningen afventer en beslutning fra ejeren

# Mixed, and separated rather than pooled. The PATTERN underneath is externally validated and keeps its own row (a scipy.special.j1 fixture to < { "0.05" } dB in tests/validate_p1_orbital_footprint.rs, plus here the published Airy half-power abscissa x = { "1.61634" } located by bisection rather than assumed). The GEOMETRY is checked against closed-form triangle trigonometry written as a different expression from the dot product under test. The CONTAINMENT of the real beam by the approximate cone is derived algebra (28.019/√η vs { "29.479" } degrees per λ/D), so the correction can only be non-positive for η ≤ { "0.9035" }. But the in-beam COUNTS themselves have no external oracle: no published table gives how many satellites of a Moonlight/LCNS-class shell hold a south-polar site inside a given dish's half-power beam, and the constellation is an illustrative public-source approximation, not a flown ephemeris. The measured disagreement at the documented working point — { "0" } links in beam under the real pattern against { "28" } under the approximation, −{ "2.33" } satellites per epoch, worst epoch { "3" }, on { "76" } evaluated links — is therefore a MODELLED finding about the approximation, pinned as a regression literal (the nearest row sits { "0.069" }° from either beam edge, four orders of magnitude above any last-digit disagreement), not an externally validated coverage number. Labelling the row ExternalDataset on the strength of the pattern's own external anchor would be borrowed validation, which is the move the matrix invariants exist to prevent. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered d9755d28), blocked (stays MODELLED): SPICE geometry and SciPy Bessel functions reproduce the off-boresight angle (worst { "3.0e-5" } deg), Airy gain and in-beam flags on { "6 336" } rows of flown-orbiter states and the documented working point; but on flown geometry the flags split by satellite identity (the nearest link is { "1.66" } deg from the beam edge), the { "5" } deg visibility is checked as a flag, not a numeric elevation, the symmetric-approximation flag applies the 31000/G rule of thumb to a validated angle without validating the rule, and the implied efficiencies have no oracle; the scoping awaits an owner decision

# An independent in-repo route. Every Jacobian row is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s evaluated from the state's own Earth-fixed and Moon-body-fixed coordinates rotated forward through the frame chain — a path that computes no derivative and shares no expression with the analytic partials — and the information matrices the two routes build agree to < { "1e-6" } of the largest diagonal. The linear-algebra kernel this composes (information_matrix, crlb, sym_eig) is separately externally anchored against numpy.linalg.eigh / numpy.linalg.inv in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs, and the delay observable carries lunar_vlbi's own delta-DOR far-field oracle; the row does not borrow either status. Closed-form identities checked here and labelled as such: σ² and 1/N scaling, and √(p/trace(M)) as an AM-HM lower bound the computed sigma cannot beat, which coincides with the equipartition value only on an isotropic geometry — which is why the measured ratio is exactly the anisotropy the assumption discarded. { "0.30" } external comparison (Library: NAIF SPICE CSPICE N0067 via spiceypy { "8.2.0" }, MIT; DE440, ITRF93 earth_latest_high_prec, MOON_PA), pre-registered ({ "50863551" }) together with an engine-side model-error sensitivity run: every Jacobian row is rebuilt by central differences of converged Newtonian light-time differences from SPICE geometry (the light-time equation is a short script over SPICE positions; SPICE carries the geometry), with the schedule rebuilt from SPICE elevations. The schedule, rank, defect, information spectrum, condition number, station sigmas, headline, trace bound, equipartition ratios, free-network null space, line-of-sight sweep and beacon declination agree within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.1" } deg, which sits above the engine known analytic-Moon error ({ "3.2e-3" } on sigmas, measured before the run). NumPy { "2.3.5" } redoes the linear algebra on the committed engine Jacobian to { "2e-13" } (P2). Validated as a Cramér-Rao bound for the stated reduced parameter set on the stated illustrative inputs: the Moon-centre ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held fixed in both legs, so it is not a real-campaign accuracy

# Closed-form identities and an independent in-repo route, with no external oracle claimed. The Helmert design — the only new derivative in the module — is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_frame_realise::apply_helmert, a path sharing no expression with the analytic form. The accumulation it composes is lunar-vlbi-fim's, whose Jacobian is finite-differenced against lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s there, and whose linear-algebra kernel is separately externally anchored against numpy in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs — this row borrows neither status. The physics oracle is structural rather than numerical: a beacon delay partial is the near-field DIFFERENCE of two near-parallel unit vectors, so the worst-determined translation direction MUST be the body-fixed direction to Earth, and the test asserts the computed answer against the separately measured direction. MEASURED: campaign-derived translation sigma { "6.3975" } m against the injected-transform scenario's { "0.3125" } m recovery error ({ "20.5" }× tighter), while rotation and scale run the OTHER way at { "2.32" }× and { "13.5" }× looser — the injected path assumes one isotropic sigma and so spreads its error uniformly across seven parameters, which the delay observable does not. Rank 7/7 but condition { "3.8e5" }, with one direction { "99.57" } % pure translation-toward-Earth and forty times worse than any other. WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT CLAIM: that the campaign figure is right in absolute terms. It establishes that the figure now DERIVES from a schedule, a geometry and an error model rather than from a planted answer. Stays ReferenceImpl/MODELLED: no published lunar-VLBI campaign-plus-datum-covariance pair exists, the station network and delay sigma are ILLUSTRATIVE inputs (the beacon catalogue is sourced, the campaign is not), observations are treated as independent while a real session's troposphere and clock are correlated between nearby scans, and the ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held FIXED. { "0.30" } external comparison, a finding (stays MODELLED): with every beacon Jacobian row rebuilt from NAIF SPICE light times (DE440, ITRF93, MOON_ME; pre-registered { "50863551" }), the schedule, Helmert rank, datum sigmas (within { "4.7e-3" }), condition, weakest direction ({ "0.07" } deg), libration sweep and arc ordering agree on six scenarios within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.2" } / { "0.05" } deg. # Internal consistency: the brute-force elevation scan, the hand-computed and orbit::dop reductions, and the closed-form Walker and geosynchronous geometry. Two-body orbits with an optional secular J2, a spherical body with the local vertical along the radius, geometric visibility only (no signal power, satellite health, terrain or third-body perturbation), and the relative phase between systems taken from different reference epochs, so it is not a snapshot of any date. { "0.30" } review, a finding (stays MODELLED): the presets meet the published one-sided DOP-availability floors (BeiDou OS Performance Standard { "3.0" }, Galileo OS SDD) at { "100" } %, but with the engine's PDOP doubled in a scratch copy they still meet them (worst point { "99.36" } %), so the floors cannot detect even a { "100" } % error in the quantity this row computes; tightening them after the run would be a post-hoc tolerance. Promotion needs a two-sided comparison of DOP values on identical geometry with a pre-set tolerance. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (tests/coverage_dop_full_oracle.rs, pre-registered add671d6), a finding (stays MODELLED): Orekit { "12.2" } positions, elevation, azimuth and ground tracks with gnss_lib_py { "1.0.4" } GDOP/PDOP/HDOP/VDOP (common clock) and a numpy inverse (one clock per constellation) agree on every bar on the Galileo, GPS, BeiDou, Galileo+GPS (one clock each) and a 1584-satellite Earth design (DOP within { "2e-14" } relative, identical fix, availability and visibility counts). On the Moon and Mars designs the elements, positions, visibility and tracks agree, but DOP exceeds the pre-set { "1e-9" } relative bar in cells whose largest PDOP is { "1434" } or more (up to { "8e-6" }), and { "4" } cells differ by one fix epoch on numerically singular geometry (engine pivot { "1e-10" } against numpy rank), so the row is not validated. { "0.30" } revisions: the TDOP/GDOP reference clock is now the lowest-numbered constellation in view (constellation-multi-gnss-coverage global GDOP max { "2.2236" } -> { "2.1205" }, mean { "1.122" } -> { "1.131" }), and per-constellation visible counts are summed as integers

# WHAT IS EXTERNALLY CHECKED, stated narrowly: against two independent open-source parsers (oracles: pycef { "1.11" } (PyPI, MIT); the Logstash CEF codec (logstash-codec-cef { "6.2.7" } in Logstash { "8.15.3" })), on a corpus of CEF lines this crate wrote ({ "157" } before amendment { "4" } below, { "170" } after it, { "176" } after amendment { "5" }): every band change and every epoch of a committed 84-line synthetic trust stream, plus adversarial text in hosts, reasons, time labels and gate names containing equals signs, backslashes, backslash-equals, pipes, line feeds, carriage returns, tabs, runs of spaces, multi-byte and astral characters and key lookalikes, extreme scores and offsets, every band with and without a previous band. pycef parses the lines whose header needs no escaping ({ "153" } before amendment { "4" }, { "166" } after it, { "172" } after amendment { "5" }) and recovers, exactly, the header fields, the severity, the exact extension key set and every value (numbers by f64 equality), after the CEF specification's unescape rules, which pycef does not apply and which scripts/gen_cef_ref.py applies. The Logstash codec, which unescapes by itself, parses every line ({ "157" }, then { "170" }, then { "176" }), meets the same bar, and also recovers four header fields that need escaping (a pipe, a backslash and an equals sign in the version string), which pycef's header pattern cannot read, so header escaping rests on the Logstash codec alone. The comparison bar was registered in commits { "5172e579" } (pycef) and 0a7598aa (Logstash) before either parser was run, and both met it at the first comparison. Five amendments are disclosed. Amendments ({ "1" }) to ({ "3" }) touched no comparison rule and no expected value: ({ "1" }) a corpus-size assertion was corrected from { "5" } to { "4" } band changes, a miscount, since the stream has { "4" }; ({ "2" }) the Logstash runner's pipeline delimiter wiring was fixed after a first attempt that produced no events; ({ "3" }) the trust score and epoch offset were moved from the CEF integer fields cn1 and cn2 to the floating-point fields cfp1 and cfp2, a wire-format change made in this release, after which the corpus was regenerated and both oracles were re-run with the bar unchanged and met it again. Amendment ({ "4" }), { "0.36" }, extended the corpus and the expected key set, registered in d6331429 before either parser ran on the new lines: schema { "1.3" } inputs add the satellite geometry in the custom fields cs6 (label geometry: status, then ';' and the deduction classes), cfp3 (geometryHdop) and cfp4 (geometryPdop); { "13" } geometry cases (the corpus to { "170" } lines) and the three new keys; both met the bar, otherwise unchanged, at the first comparison (pycef { "166" } of { "170" } lines parsed, the { "4" } tier { "2" } header cases as before; Logstash { "170" } of { "170" }). Amendment ({ "5" }), { "0.36" }, appended { "6" } geometry-label cases (the corpus to { "176" } lines), registered in 9a8e4fa9 before either parser ran on them: from inputs that carry the geometry label, cs6 gains a third field (';' and the label) and cn1 (geometryUsedSatellites) the satellites used; the Logstash codec's mapping table was read first (no flexString fields, hence cs6 rather than flexString1); both met the bar at the first comparison (pycef { "172" } of { "176" }, Logstash { "176" } of { "176" }), and every earlier reference entry was reproduced unchanged. WHAT IS NOT: neither parser type-checks the cfp values (both keep them as text), so the use of the floating-point fields rests on the CEF dictionary rather than on an oracle; no vendor SIEM was run; the extension key names and their mapping are this crate's choice, and the parsers show only that they are read back as written; the RFC { "5424" } envelope, delivery, timeouts and reconnection are not covered; pycef is a small parser last released in { "2020" }, and the Logstash codec is the more substantial oracle.
seg-59fd23d6607e = HVAD DER KONTROLLERES EKSTERNT, angivet snævert: mod to uafhængige open source-parsere (referencer: pycef { "1.11" } (PyPI, MIT); Logstash CEF-codec (logstash-codec-cef { "6.2.7" } i Logstash { "8.15.3" })), på et korpus af CEF-linjer, som dette crate skrev ({ "157" } før tillæg { "4" } nedenfor, { "170" } efter det, { "176" } efter tillæg { "5" }): hver båndændring og hver epoke i en committet syntetisk tillidsstrøm på 84 linjer, plus modstridig tekst i værter, årsager, tidsetiketter og gatenavne med lighedstegn, omvendte skråstreger, omvendt skråstreg-lighedstegn, lodrette streger, linjeskift, vognretur, tabulatorer, rækker af mellemrum, flerbytetegn og astrale tegn og nøgler, der ligner hinanden, ekstreme scorer og forskydninger, hvert bånd med og uden et foregående bånd. pycef parser de linjer, hvis header ikke kræver escaping ({ "153" } før tillæg { "4" }, { "166" } efter det, { "172" } efter tillæg { "5" }), og genfinder nøjagtigt headerfelterne, alvorsgraden, det nøjagtige sæt af extension-nøgler og hver værdi (tal ved f64-lighed), efter CEF-specifikationens regler for unescaping, som pycef ikke anvender, og som scripts/gen_cef_ref.py anvender. # checked against its own closed-form / analytic identity — catches transcription and coefficient errors, but is not an external oracle — Closed-form identities and internal consistency; NO external oracle is claimed. The limb half angle is checked against asin(R_earth/r_orbit) and the geometry against it; the free-space loss, the DOP kernel and the SGP4 propagation this composes are each externally validated in their own rows and this row does not borrow their status. WHY MODELLED, and it is the transmit pattern: the gain at angle is a uniformly illuminated circular aperture, the Airy pattern, while a real GPS L1 antenna is a twelve-element helical array with a shaped main lobe peaked off-boresight to even out power across the Earth disc and sidelobes that are not Airy. Published measured patterns exist and are not vendored here, so orderings and orders of magnitude carry, while the dB of any single satellite does not. A FIRST IMPLEMENTATION OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING: the Airy expression is valid only in the forward hemisphere, its argument goes as sin(theta), and at theta near { "180" } deg it wraps around and returns FULL BORESIGHT GAIN behind the aperture - so the two strongest links in the first report were satellites pointing their antennas away from the Moon, and they were the only two that cleared the threshold. Behind the aperture the model now applies a flat back-lobe floor, stated as a bound rather than a fabricated pattern shape. Deliberately absent, each making the budget OPTIMISTIC: no ionospheric or tropospheric loss on the limb-grazing rays, no polarisation, pointing or implementation loss, and a spherical Earth with no refractive extension. The Moon position is an INPUT, not an ephemeris lookup, because the quantity under test is the link and the beam geometry. Upgrading this row to Validated needs a measured transmit pattern and LuGRE normal points to check against; neither is in the repository and neither is invented here. { "0.30" } round { "2" }, a finding (stays MODELLED): a measured-input link path (real SP3 positions, per-block transmit power, tabulated transmit and receive patterns, acquisition and tracking hysteresis) was checked against Montenbruck et al. { "2023" } Table { "3" } for GENESIS at { "6000" } km (pre-registered 5b414f0a): zenith-antenna means agree on { "5" } of { "6" } within one dB of the paper sensitivity; nadir-antenna means are { "1.0" } to { "2.4" } satellites higher than printed, plausibly because the paper used azimuth-dependent GPS side-lobe patterns that are not published. The lunar-distance figures of this row still use the Airy stand-in. { "0.30" }+ D7, a finding (stays MODELLED): the restated quantity, the C/N0 DIFFERENCE between Block IIR/IIR-M satellites tracked at one epoch, predicted with the NAVCEN per-SVN measured L1 patterns (two-dimensional, yaw-steering frame) against the LuGRE flight C/N0 (pre-registered 768cb62b: { "99" } % visible, pair RMS <= { "3.0" } dB, median <= { "2.0" } dB, at least { "50" } records and { "30" } pairs) has too little data: the { "26" } qualifying transit and lunar-orbit epochs hold { "15" } tracked IIR/IIR-M records (mostly IIF and III are tracked) and one pair. # checked against its own closed-form / analytic identity — catches transcription and coefficient errors, but is not an external oracle — Two internal oracles, no external dataset. ({ "1" }) Closed-form round trip: the minimum detectable bias is produced by inverting the non-central chi-square tail on the non-centrality (raim::pbias) and is verified by feeding the resulting non-centrality back through raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf at the monitor's own threshold, which must return P_md. ({ "2" }) Injection vs analysis: a bias is written into the RF estimate of one axis and cross_raim::run_cross_raim is re-run, so the statistic compared against the analytic non-centrality is the one the monitor computed, not a re-derivation. A seeded Monte-Carlo of the statistic itself is carried in the tests as a third check; it is deliberately NOT in the report, because a sampled estimate would be slower and not reproducible bit-for-bit. ExternalDataset is declined: the quantity is the detection power of THIS monitor at THIS scenario's sigma allocation, and the sigma magnitudes the MDB is scaled by are Modelled representative inputs, not measurements. ReferenceImpl was also considered and declined — the Monte-Carlo samples the same statistic the analysis describes, so it catches transcription and coefficient errors but is not an independent implementation of the monitor. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered 0135a9e4), blocked (stays MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduce the report threshold (within { "5.4e-12" }), per-axis MDB, power curve, crossing multiple and ramp time-to-detect (within { "5.3e-10" }) on five configurations; the injected-fault statistics have no independent oracle and the sigma magnitudes are Modelled inputs, so the scoping awaits an owner decision

# checked against a separate implementation in this same codebase — independent of the unit under test, but not externally authoritative — Closed-form identities and an independent in-repo route, with no external oracle claimed. The Helmert design — the only new derivative in the module — is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_frame_realise::apply_helmert, a path sharing no expression with the analytic form. The accumulation it composes is lunar-vlbi-fim's, whose Jacobian is finite-differenced against lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s there, and whose linear-algebra kernel is separately externally anchored against numpy in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs — this row borrows neither status. The physics oracle is structural rather than numerical: a beacon delay partial is the near-field DIFFERENCE of two near-parallel unit vectors, so the worst-determined translation direction MUST be the body-fixed direction to Earth, and the test asserts the computed answer against the separately measured direction. MEASURED: campaign-derived translation sigma { "6.3975" } m against the injected-transform scenario's { "0.3125" } m recovery error ({ "20.5" }× tighter), while rotation and scale run the OTHER way at { "2.32" }× and { "13.5" }× looser — the injected path assumes one isotropic sigma and so spreads its error uniformly across seven parameters, which the delay observable does not. Rank 7/7 but condition { "3.8e5" }, with one direction { "99.57" } % pure translation-toward-Earth and forty times worse than any other. WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT CLAIM: that the campaign figure is right in absolute terms. It establishes that the figure now DERIVES from a schedule, a geometry and an error model rather than from a planted answer. Stays ReferenceImpl/MODELLED: no published lunar-VLBI campaign-plus-datum-covariance pair exists, the station network and delay sigma are ILLUSTRATIVE inputs (the beacon catalogue is sourced, the campaign is not), observations are treated as independent while a real session's troposphere and clock are correlated between nearby scans, and the ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held FIXED. { "0.30" } external comparison, a finding (stays MODELLED): with every beacon Jacobian row rebuilt from NAIF SPICE light times (DE440, ITRF93, MOON_ME; pre-registered { "50863551" }), the schedule, Helmert rank, datum sigmas (within { "4.7e-3" }), condition, weakest direction ({ "0.07" } deg), libration sweep and arc ordering agree on six scenarios within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.2" } / { "0.05" } deg. NumPy on the committed engine Jacobian agrees to { "3e-14" } where the stations are fixed. With the stations estimated it differs by { "6.8e-6" } against a pre-registered { "1e-9" }, whose condition-number premise was wrong (Helmert condition { "2.1e8" }; independent NumPy routes disagree by { "3e-6" }; a contribution from the engine { "1e-9" } pseudo-inverse fallback is not ruled out), so the row is not promoted on that pre-registration. { "0.36" } re-registration at a condition-scaled bar (pre-registered b887eb05; t = max({ "1e-12" }, { "10" } kappa { "2" }^-53) from the conditioning of the joint information and its Helmert reduction), a finding (stays MODELLED, NOT MET): step { "0" } rules the pseudo-inverse fallback OUT for the { "6.8e-6" } gap (no matrix upstream of the Helmert matrix falls back on the six original scenarios, tightest margin a factor { "2.6" }), the SPICE leg still holds on all six, and NumPy agrees within the bar on six of eight scenarios (default, the two arcs, the 3x delay sigma, the collinear defect and the fresh stations-fixed run on 2026-12-01; worst { "0.13" } of its bar). It fails on stations_estimated (datum sigmas up to { "6.8e-6" }, condition { "9.1e-6" }, independence-discarded norm { "3.5e-6" }, against t = { "5.9e-7" }), and on the fresh date the south-pole beacon has no observation, so the beacon information is rank-deficient (pseudo-inverse fallback upstream of the Helmert matrix in both fresh scenarios) and the unchanged NumPy oracle cannot evaluate stations_estimated_fresh (singular matrix). # <a1>kshana.dev/studio</a1> gains a Bridge replay (two runs side by side on one mission clock, at <a2><c1/></a2>), an Optical PNT page (<c2/>), a Holdover test page (<c3/>) and an observatory viewer (<c4/>). The pictures above are drawn from the engine's own output by a script; they are not screenshots of these pages.
seg-5c17e7d00e39 = <a1>kshana.dev/studio</a1> får en Bridge-afspilning (to kørsler side om side på ét missionsur, på <a2><c1/></a2>), en side for optisk PNT (<c2/>), en side for holdover-test (<c3/>) og en observatorievisning (<c4/>). De kører den samme motor i browseren, intet uploades, og de bærer den samme vejledende erklæring. Billederne ovenfor er tegnet af et script ud fra motorens eget output; de er ikke skærmbilleder af disse sider.

# <c1/>, <c2/>: a seeded Monte Carlo (N = { "20,000" } epochs per geometry, σ = { "1.5" } m, ChaCha8 seed { "36" } + i) of <c3/> with unit weights, solved from the true position, on the <b>{ "8" } geometries of <c4/></b> (the ones whose DOP is matched to gnss_lib_py), against σ²·Q where Q is the covariance factor of the <c5/> kernel itself (<c6/>, the same arithmetic). <b>Tolerances pre-registered</b> (commit e50c88d2, before the first run, per the { "0.36" } geometry design note, section { "5.2" }), each a number of standard errors: east, north, up and clock variances within { "5" } %; H/V/P RMS within { "2.5" } %; east-north correlation within { "0.035" }; means within { "5" } standard errors; the { "95" } % horizontal-ellipse fraction within { "0.95" } ± { "0.0077" }. First run: all { "104" } checks pass (worst variance { "3.2" } %, RMS { "1.6" } %, correlation { "0.012" }, mean { "2.2" } standard errors, ellipse { "0.0039" }). <b>Negative controls</b> fail as required: PDOP doubled fails every geometry; errors growing as 1/sin(elevation) against the unweighted Q fail a variance check on every geometry whose lowest satellite is below { "20" }°. <b>How it composes with the row above:</b> that row validates these geometries' DOP against gnss_lib_py; this one adds the step from those factors to metres through the engine's solver. <b>A first, separate check is kept beside it</b> (<c7/>, <c8/>, run before the pre-registration and reported as such): four hand-chosen skies (open, clustered, canyon, half-sky; σ { "1" } m) with chi-square confidence intervals at { "1e-6" } total over { "20" } checks; ratios { "0.987" } to { "1.009" }, and its own negative controls (a { "10" } % DOP error, 1/sin(elevation) errors) fail as required. <b>Why MODELLED:</b> no external dataset; it does not show that a real receiver's error follows the model, and σ_UERE is an input.
seg-fc3443210358 = <c1/>, <c2/>: en Monte Carlo med seed (N = { "20,000" } epoker pr. geometri, σ = { "1.5" } m, ChaCha8-seed { "36" } + i) af <c3/> med enhedsvægte, løst fra den sande position, på <b>de { "8" } geometrier i <c4/></b> (dem hvis DOP er matchet med gnss_lib_py), mod σ²·Q, hvor Q er kovariansfaktoren for selve kernen <c5/> (<c6/>, den samme aritmetik). <b>Tolerancer præregistreret</b> (commit e50c88d2, før den første kørsel, efter designnotatet for geometri for { "0.36" }, afsnit { "5.2" }), hver et antal standardfejl: varianser for øst, nord, op og ur inden for { "5" } %; RMS for H/V/P inden for { "2.5" } %; korrelation mellem øst og nord inden for { "0.035" }; middelværdier inden for { "5" } standardfejl; andelen i den horisontale ellipse på { "95" } % inden for { "0.95" } ± { "0.0077" }. # <b>What the observatory does instead.</b> The question the almanac comparison answers is "could the constellation geometry alone explain this low accuracy?". The base method answers a related question without an almanac: a low share must stand out against the same day's background across the region and against the aircraft's own good reports elsewhere. That is a different and weaker substitute: it cannot say that a quiet-geometry hour was degraded where the day's background is also low. An almanac-HDOP scorer is <b>not implemented</b>. It is an open item, to be built from the paper's full text and the public GPS almanac and pre-registered before it is run; until then it is cited, not claimed.
seg-4f8471649a07 = <b>Hvad observatoriet gør i stedet.</b> Spørgsmålet, som almanaksammenligningen besvarer, er: "kan konstellationsgeometrien alene forklare denne lave nøjagtighed?". Grundmetoden besvarer et beslægtet spørgsmål uden almanak: en lav andel skal skille sig ud mod samme dags baggrund i regionen og mod flyets egne gode rapporter andre steder. Det er en anden og svagere erstatning: den kan ikke sige, at en time med rolig geometri var forringet, hvor dagens baggrund også er lav. En almanak-HDOP-scorer er <b>ikke implementeret</b>. Det er et åbent punkt, der skal bygges ud fra artiklens fulde tekst og den offentlige GPS-almanak og præregistreres, før den køres; indtil da er den citeret, ikke hævdet.
